[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import --- Grade C or Grade Nest Run

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder who
imports eggs that are graded Grade C or Grade Nest Run must deliver them
directly to an establishment where eggs are processed and treated by a
licence holder.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import --- ungraded eggs

- **[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 306(1), a licence
  holder may import ungraded eggs if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the import, notify the Minister in writing
    of the quantity of ungraded eggs that are intended to be imported,
    the date of the import and the name of the licence holder and
    address of the establishment referred to in paragraph (c);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} package them in a container that is labelled with
    the expression "Ungraded Eggs" or "[oeufs non classifiés]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}"; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} deliver them directly to an establishment where
    eggs are processed and treated by a licence holder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal --- imported ungraded eggs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any imported ungraded eggs delivered to an
  establishment referred to in paragraph (1)(c) may be removed from that
  establishment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the eggs have been processed and treated by a
    licence holder; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the eggs are delivered directly to another
    establishment where eggs are processed and treated by a licence
    holder.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interprovincial trade

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person who sends or conveys any of the following from one province to
  another must deliver them to an establishment where eggs are processed
  and treated by a licence holder:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} eggs that are graded Canada A or Canada B that bear
    an ink mark that consists of the word "dyed" or "[teint]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}" or a deposit of ink that is applied to an egg's shell by
    the holder of a licence to grade eggs;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} eggs that are graded Canada C;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} eggs that are graded Grade C or Grade Nest Run that
    are imported; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ungraded eggs that are imported in accordance with
    subsection 98(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interprovincial trade --- Canada Nest
  Run

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person who sends or conveys eggs that are graded
  Canada Nest Run from one province to another must deliver them to an
  establishment where either eggs are graded or eggs are processed and
  treated by a licence holder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interprovincial trade --- ungraded eggs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 306(1), any person may send or
  convey from one province to another ungraded eggs, other than eggs
  that are rejected in accordance with subsection 333(1) or eggs that
  are imported in accordance with subsection 98(1), if the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} packages them in a container that is labelled with
    the expression "Ungraded Eggs" or "[oeufs non classifiés]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}"; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} delivers them to an establishment where either eggs
    are graded or eggs are processed and treated by a licence holder.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ink

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** If a licence holder
applies ink to an egg's shell, the ink must be fast-drying and indelible
and it must not present a risk of injury to human health.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trays

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** Before sending plastic
trays to an egg producer, a licence holder must clean, sanitize and dry
them.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Processed Egg Products]{.HTitleText2} {#h-844537 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processing and treating eggs

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder may process and treat eggs only if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are edible;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} do not emit an abnormal odour;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are not mouldy;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} have not been in an incubator;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} do not have an internal defect, other than a
    particle of the oviduct or a blood spot neither of which exceeds 3
    mm in diameter;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} are not leakers, except if they become leakers
    while being transferred to the egg-breaking equipment and they are
    prepared in a manner that prevents the contamination of the
    processed egg product; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} are free from dirt and other foreign matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processing and treating processed egg
  products

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A licence holder may process and treat processed egg
  products only if they are derived from eggs that meet the requirements
  of paragraphs (1)(a) to (g).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temperature

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  processed egg product that is to be sent or conveyed from one province
  to another or exported and that is processed or treated in an
  establishment that is identified in a licence must have been chilled
  to 4°C or less before being removed from the establishment, if it is a

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} liquid whole egg;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liquid yolk;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} liquid egg white or liquid albumen;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} liquid whole egg mix;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} liquid yolk mix; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} liquid egg product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Minister may, in writing,
  authorize any person to remove a processed egg product that has not
  been chilled to 4°C or less if the Minister is of the opinion that no
  risk of injury to human health will result.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import --- foreign official document

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** The holder of a licence
to import may import a processed egg product only if the licence holder
provides an inspector with an official document issued by the foreign
state, in a form approved by the President, that states that the
processed egg product meets the requirements that are set out in the Act
and these Regulations.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Fish]{.HTitleText2} {#h-844563 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- import of mitten crab or
puffer fish

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is prohibited for a person to import

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a live, freshwater mitten crab of the genus
    [Eriocheir]{.otherLang lang="la"}; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a puffer fish of the family
    [Tetraodontidae]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- personal use

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 19 of the Act does not apply in respect of an
  import referred to in subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import of live or raw shellfish

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a licence to import may import live or raw shellfish only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign state in which the shellfish is
    harvested, manufactured, prepared, stored, packaged or labelled, as
    the case may be, has, at the time the activity is conducted, an
    inspection system for the shellfish that is recognized under Part 7;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign state from which the shellfish is
    imported has, at the time of the import, an inspection system for
    shellfish that is recognized under Part 7; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any establishment where the shellfish was
    manufactured, prepared, stored, packaged or labelled has, at the
    time that the activity is conducted and at the time of the import, a
    system for manufacturing, preparing, storing, packaging or
    labelling, as the case may be, that complies with an inspection
    system for shellfish that is recognized under Part 7.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The conditions in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) do not
  apply in respect of the import of the adductor muscles of scallops or
  the meat of geoducks.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shellfish

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder may manufacture, prepare, store, package or label
  shellfish that is to be exported or sent or conveyed from one province
  to another only if the shellfish is harvested in an area

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that has been classified under the Canadian
    Shellfish Sanitation Program and that is not subject to an order
    prohibiting fishing issued under the [Management of Contaminated
    Fisheries Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-90-351); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is subject to an order prohibiting fishing
    issued under the [Management of Contaminated Fisheries
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-90-351) but the shellfish has been
    decontaminated in accordance with the decontamination plan submitted
    in connection with a licence to fish for food purposes issued under
    those Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The conditions in paragraphs (1)(a) and (b) do not
  apply in respect of the manufacturing, preparing, storing, packaging
  or labelling of the adductor muscles of scallops or the meat of
  geoducks.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frozen fish

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
protect from dehydration and oxidation all frozen fish that is stored in
a conveyance.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Fresh Fruits or Vegetables]{.HTitleText2} {#h-844588 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION A]{.HLabel3}[Interpretation and Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844589 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[apple]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[apple]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fresh apple for which a grade
    is prescribed by these Regulations. ([pomme]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[onion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[onion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fresh onion for which a grade
    is prescribed by these Regulations. ([oignon]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[potato]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[potato]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fresh potato for which a
    grade is prescribed by these Regulations. ([pomme de
    terre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fresh fruits or vegetables packaged
together

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** The requirements of
sections 113 to 121 and 269 --- as well as any requirements under
Division 2 of Part 10 and Part 12 that apply in respect of fresh fruits
or vegetables --- do not apply in respect of consumer prepackaged fresh
fruits or vegetables if the container contains more than one type of
fresh fruit or vegetable but no other food and if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the label that is applied or attached to the
  container bears the expression "Fresh Pack" or "[emballage
  frais]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or, in the case of consumer prepackaged
  fresh vegetables, the expression "Stew-pack" or "[légumes
  mixtes]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or the expression "Vegetables for Stew"
  or "[légumes pour ragoût]{.otherLang lang="fr"}";

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no one type of fresh fruit or vegetable in the
  container exceeds 1 kg net weight; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the net weight of the fresh fruits or vegetables in
  the container does not exceed 10 kg.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fresh fruits or vegetables packaged with
other food

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** The requirements of
sections 113 to 121 and 269 --- as well as any requirements under
Division 2 of Part 10 and Part 12 that apply in respect of fresh fruits
or vegetables --- do not apply in respect of consumer prepackaged fresh
fruits or vegetables if the container contains more than one type of
fresh fruit or vegetable together with other food and if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the label that is applied or attached to the
  container bears the expression "Gift Pack" or
  "[emballage-cadeau]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or the expression "Combo
  Pack" or "[emballage mixte]{.otherLang lang="fr"}";

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no one type of fresh fruit or vegetable in the
  container exceeds 1 kg net weight; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the net weight of the fresh fruits or vegetables and
  other food in the container does not exceed 10 kg.

#### [SUBDIVISION B]{.HLabel3}[Import]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844605 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Whole fresh fruits or vegetables

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** The requirements of
this Subdivision apply in respect of any fresh fruits or vegetables that
are whole.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imported potatoes

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Potatoes that are imported must meet
  the requirements for the grade Canada No. 1 that are set out in the
  Compendium.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption --- potatoes from United
  States

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Potatoes that are imported from the United States are
  considered to meet the requirements for the grade Canada No. 1 that
  are set out in the Compendium if the potatoes have been graded in the
  United States and meet the applicable requirements that are set out in
  the document entitled Grade Standard Requirements for Fresh Fruits or
  Vegetables Imported from the United States, prepared by the Agency and
  published on its website, as amended from time to time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Apples from foreign state other than
United States

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Apples that are imported from a
  foreign state other than the United States must meet the requirements
  for the grade Canada Extra Fancy, Canada Fancy or Canada Commercial
  that are set out in the Compendium.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Apples from United States

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Apples that are imported from the United States must
  meet the requirements for the grade Canada Extra Fancy or Canada Fancy
  that are set out in the Compendium.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption --- apples from United
  States

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Apples that are imported from the United States are
  considered to meet the requirements for the grade Canada Extra Fancy
  or Canada Fancy that are set out in the Compendium if the apples have
  been graded in the United States and meet the applicable requirements
  that are set out in the document entitled Grade Standard Requirements
  for Fresh Fruits or Vegetables Imported from the United States,
  prepared by the Agency and published on its website, as amended from
  time to time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption --- general

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** Fresh fruits or
vegetables, other than potatoes or apples, that are imported from the
United States are considered to meet the applicable requirements that
are set out in the Compendium if the fruits or vegetables have been
graded in the United States and meet the applicable requirements that
are set out in the document entitled Grade Standard Requirements for
Fresh Fruits or Vegetables Imported from the United States, prepared by
the Agency and published on its website, as amended from time to time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign states --- onions, potatoes and
apples

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** Onions and potatoes
that are imported from a foreign state other than the United States, and
apples that are imported from a foreign state other than the United
States and New Zealand, must meet and be certified by the Minister as
meeting the following requirements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicable requirements that are set out in Parts
  10 to 12;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of onions, the requirements for a
  particular grade of onions that are set out in the Compendium;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of potatoes, the requirements for the
  grade Canada No. 1 that are set out in the Compendium; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of apples, the requirements for the grade
  Canada Extra Fancy, Canada Fancy or Canada Commercial that are set out
  in the Compendium.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Onions, potatoes and apples from United
States

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Onions, potatoes and apples that are
  imported from the United States must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied at the Canadian port of entry by a
    serially numbered certificate or evidence, in the form of a
    facsimile or a copy of an email message, issued by the federal
    department responsible for agriculture in the United States, that
    establishes that the following requirements are met:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the applicable requirements that are set out in
      Parts 10 to 12,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of onions, the requirements for a
      particular grade of onions that are set out in the Compendium,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of potatoes, the requirements for
      the grade Canada No. 1 that are set out in the Compendium, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in the case of apples, the requirements for the
      grade Canada Extra Fancy or Canada Fancy that are set out in the
      Compendium; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet and be certified by the Minister as meeting
    the requirements that are set out in subparagraph (a)(i) and, in
    accordance with any general tolerances for inspection at the time of
    shipping or repackaging that are set out in the Compendium, the
    requirements that are set out in subparagraph (a)(ii), (iii) or
    (iv).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certificate and evidence referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) must be endorsed with the expression "Meets Canadian Import
  Requirements for Grades, Packaging, Labelling and Standard Container
  Size" or "[satisfait aux exigences d'importation du Canada visant la
  classification, l'emballage, l'étiquetage et la taille des contenants
  normalisés]{.otherLang lang="fr"}".

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Apples from New Zealand

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Apples that are imported from New
  Zealand must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied at the Canadian port of entry by a
    serially numbered certificate or evidence, in the form of a
    facsimile or a copy of an email message, issued by the ministry
    responsible for agriculture in New Zealand, that establishes that
    the following requirements are met:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the applicable requirements that are set out in
      Parts 10 to 12, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the requirements for the grade Canada Extra
      Fancy, Canada Fancy or Canada Commercial that are set out in the
      Compendium; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet and be certified by the Minister as meeting
    the requirements that are set out in subparagraph (a)(i) and, in
    accordance with any general tolerances for inspection at the time of
    shipping or repackaging that are set out in the Compendium, the
    requirements that are set out in subparagraph (a)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certificate and evidence referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) must be endorsed with the expression "Meets Canadian Import
  Requirements for Grades, Packaging, Labelling and Standard Container
  Size" or "[satisfait aux exigences d'importation du Canada visant la
  classification, l'emballage, l'étiquetage et la taille des contenants
  normalisés]{.otherLang lang="fr"}".

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** Sections 116 to 118 do
not apply in respect of onions, potatoes or apples that are part of a
shipment that consists of not more than

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} 15 containers of onions with an aggregate net weight
  of not more than 250 kg of onions;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 15 containers of potatoes with an aggregate net
  weight of not more than 250 kg of potatoes; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} 15 containers of apples with an aggregate net weight
  of not more than 250 kg of apples.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In transit

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 113 to 119, if fresh fruits or vegetables are sent or conveyed
to Canada in an in bond shipment from a foreign state other than the
United States and pass only in transit through the United States, the
fresh fruits or vegetables are not considered to have been imported from
the United States.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for certificate

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application for the issuance of a certificate referred to in section
  116 or paragraph 117(1)(b) or 118(1)(b) must be made to the Minister
  in a form approved by the President.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may require an inspection for the
  purpose of deciding whether to issue the certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection --- accessibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an inspection is required, the applicant must make
  the onions, potatoes or apples readily accessible to an inspector at
  the time of inspection.

#### [SUBDIVISION C]{.HLabel3}[Trade of Fresh Fruits or Vegetables]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844662 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is prohibited for a person to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sell any fresh fruits or vegetables that are to be
    exported or sent or conveyed from one province to another;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} purchase or negotiate the purchase on another
    person's behalf of any fresh fruits or vegetables that are to be
    imported or sent or conveyed from one province to another;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} receive any fresh fruits or vegetables that have
    been imported or sent or conveyed from one province to another; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} send or convey from one province to another or
    import or export any fresh fruits or vegetables.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person who is a member in good standing of the
    Fruit and Vegetable Dispute Resolution Corporation, a corporation
    incorporated under Part 2 of the [Canada Not-for-profit Corporations
    Act](/eng/acts/C-7.75), as described in its by-laws;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person who only sells fresh fruits or
    vegetables directly to consumers if that person paid less than
    \$100,000 for the fresh fruits and vegetables that they sold to
    consumers within the previous 12 months;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who only purchases, sells or negotiates
    the purchase or sale on another person's behalf, sends or conveys
    from one province to another or imports or exports less than one
    metric ton of fresh fruits and vegetables per day;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any person who only sells fresh fruits or
    vegetables that they have grown themselves; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a [[registered charity]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as
    defined in subsection 248(1) of the [Income Tax
    Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) or a club, society or association described in
    paragraph 149(1)(l) of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- nuts, wild fruit and wild
  vegetables

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of nuts,
  wild fruit and wild vegetables.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damaged or defective fresh fruits or
vegetables

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  claim is made that all or part of a shipment of fresh fruits or
  vegetables that is imported or sent or conveyed from one province to
  another has arrived at its destination in a damaged or defective
  condition, any person who has a financial interest in that shipment
  may make a request to the Minister for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written report that describes the condition of
    any damaged or defective fruits or vegetables at the time of the
    inspection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written notice of the disposition of any damaged
    or defective fresh fruits or vegetables that describes the amount of
    fresh fruit or vegetables that have been disposed of and the place
    and time of their disposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for report or notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request for a report or notice referred to in
  subsection (1) must be made to the Minister in a form approved by the
  President.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection --- accessibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person who makes the request for a report
  regarding the condition of fresh fruits or vegetables must make the
  fresh fruits or vegetables readily accessible to an inspector at the
  time of inspection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of fresh fruit or vegetable

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person who makes the request for a notice
  regarding the disposition of damaged or defective fresh fruits or
  vegetables must ensure that their disposition is witnessed by an
  inspector.

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Meat Products and Food Animals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-844690 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION A]{.HLabel3}[Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844691 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[on-farm food safety program]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[on-farm food safety program]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a program
    in respect of food animals that is operated on a farm or at a
    similar place and under which hazards relating to the safety of meat
    products that may be derived from those food animals are identified,
    analyzed and controlled. ([programme de salubrité des aliments à la
    ferme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[specified risk material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[specified risk material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
    as in section 6.1 of the [Health of Animals
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._296). ([matériel à risque
    spécifié]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[split]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[split]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to cut a carcass lengthwise
    along the median line. ([fendre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[veterinary inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[veterinary inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
    designated as a veterinary inspector under subsection 13(3) of the
    [Canadian Food Inspection Agency Act](/eng/acts/C-16.5) for the
    purposes of the Act. ([inspecteur vétérinaire]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

#### [SUBDIVISION B]{.HLabel3}[Edible Meat Products]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844698 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification of meat products as edible

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder may identify a meat product as edible only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the food animal from which the meat product is
    derived, or a sample from the shipment that the food animal is part
    of, is subjected to an ante-mortem examination under section 138;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the food animal, other than a game animal, from
    which the meat product is derived, or a sample from the shipment
    that the food animal is part of, is subjected to an ante-mortem
    inspection under section 139;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the carcass of the food animal from which the meat
    product is derived is dressed or partially dressed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the carcass, its parts and the blood of the food
    animal from which the meat product is derived are subjected to a
    post-mortem inspection under subsection 149(1) or a post-mortem
    examination under subsection 150(1); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the meat product is edible and is not contaminated,
    including that it does not contain any specified risk material.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain meat products --- additional
  requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite (1), a licence holder must not identify as
  edible any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a heart --- other than the heart of a domesticated
    rabbit or of a bird that is not an ostrich, rhea or emu --- unless
    it is opened or inverted and has all blood clots and attached blood
    vessels removed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a liver, unless the gallbladder is removed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a gizzard, unless its contents and lining are
    removed and the gizzard is washed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a meat product that contains a urinary bladder, an
    intestine or any part of a urinary bladder or intestine, unless the
    bladder, intestine or part is used as a natural casing for the meat
    product and meets the requirements of section 126; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a meat product with an artificial casing, unless
    the casing is manufactured from a material that is free of any
    noxious constituent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Natural casings

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** A urinary bladder, an
intestine or any part of a urinary bladder or intestine may be used as a
natural casing for an edible meat product if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the bladder, the intestine or the part of the bladder
  or intestine have been emptied of their contents and washed, and their
  mucous lining has been removed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a bladder, it was inverted and placed
  in brine for at least 12 hours and was subsequently rinsed with water;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the casing is clean.

#### [SUBDIVISION C]{.HLabel3}[Humane Treatment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844719 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidable death --- clarification

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
a reference to an avoidable death in these Regulations does not include
the slaughtering of a food animal in accordance with these Regulations
or the humane killing of a food animal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidable suffering, injury or death

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
handle a food animal at the establishment in a manner that does not
cause it avoidable suffering, injury or death and must not subject it to
any condition that may cause such suffering, injury or death.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hitting

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must not hit a food animal with a whip, prod or, except
  for the purposes of section 141, any other object.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electric prod

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A licence holder must not apply an electric prod to a
  food animal unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is done for the purpose of causing the animal to
    move and there is no reasonably practicable alternative to the
    application of the prod;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the food animal is a pig or a bovine;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the prod is applied to the lateral portion of the
    rear leg muscles between the hock joint and the hip joint;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the food animal has sufficient space to move
    forward;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the food animal's ability to move is not
    compromised; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the prod is applied in a manner that does not cause
    the food animal avoidable suffering, injury or death.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessing

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must assess whether a food animal is showing signs of
  suffering or injury on its arrival at the establishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After a food animal's arrival, the licence holder
  must monitor it on a regular basis, including assessing the conditions
  to which the food animal is subjected in the establishment that may
  cause avoidable suffering, injury or death.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective action

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the licence holder determines that conditions
  exist that may cause avoidable suffering, injury or death to a food
  animal, the licence holder must immediately take corrective action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suffering -- immediate measures

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a food animal is showing signs of suffering, the
  licence holder must immediately

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} alleviate its suffering;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} humanely kill it; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} slaughter it in accordance with these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- game animals

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of a game
  animal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Game animals

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder who
has direct control over a game animal must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} monitor it on a regular basis, including assessing
  the conditions to which the game animal is subjected in the
  establishment that may cause avoidable suffering, injury or death;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} immediately take corrective action if such conditions
  exist; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the game animal is showing signs of suffering,
  immediately

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} alleviate its suffering,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} humanely kill it, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} slaughter it in accordance with these
    Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Segregation and isolation

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} segregate food animals of different species;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} segregate a sick or injured food animal with other
  sick or injured food animals or isolate a sick or injured food animal
  if, because of its condition, it presents a risk to other food animals
  or it requires protection from other food animals; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} isolate a food animal that may cause suffering,
  injury or death to other food animals because of its nature,
  temperament, gender, weight, age or any other cause.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overcrowding

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
provide a food animal with sufficient space to prevent the suffering of,
injury to or death of the animal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ventilation

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
provide a food animal with sufficient ventilation to prevent the
suffering of, injury to or death of the animal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Handling

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder who handles a food animal, including by handling a
  crate containing a food animal, during any activity they conduct in
  the establishment, must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be able to do so, by reason of the person's
    competencies and qualifications for the activity, without causing
    avoidable suffering, injury or death to the food animal; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} do so in a manner and under circumstances in which
    the equipment that is used will not cause avoidable suffering,
    injury or death to the food animal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas of establishment and equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A licence holder must, during any activity conducted
  by the licence holder, use only areas of an establishment and
  equipment for the handling of a food animal that are designed,
  constructed and maintained in such a manner that they will not cause
  avoidable suffering, injury or death to the food animal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Water and feed

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must provide a food animal --- other than a food animal
  that is confined in a crate --- that is unloaded from a conveyance at
  an establishment with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} water or another source of hydration as soon as it
    is unloaded; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} feed, within 24 hours after it is unloaded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In crate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a food animal that is confined in a
  crate, the licence holder must provide the food animal with water or
  another source of hydration and with feed within 24 hours after it
  arrives at the establishment.

#### [SUBDIVISION D]{.HLabel3}[Removal and Keeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844780 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must notify a veterinary inspector before a food animal
  is removed from an establishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A licence holder must notify a veterinary inspector
  before keeping a food animal in an establishment for more than seven
  days.

#### [SUBDIVISION E]{.HLabel3}[Ante-mortem Examination and Inspection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844786 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ante-mortem examination

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 24 hours before the slaughter
  of a food animal and in accordance with the document entitled
  Ante-mortem Examination and Presentation Procedures for Food Animals,
  prepared by the Agency and published on its website, as amended from
  time to time, a licence holder must conduct an ante-mortem examination
  of the food animal or of a sample from the shipment that the food
  animal is part of, which must include, in the case an equine or a bird
  other than one that is a game animal or an ostrich, a rhea or an emu,
  the examination of the documents referred to in subsection 165(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deviations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the licence holder, in the course of the
  ante-mortem examination or at any other time before slaughter,
  suspects that the food animal shows a deviation from normal behaviour,
  physiology or appearance, the licence holder must hold it for an
  inspection by a veterinary inspector, unless otherwise authorized by a
  veterinary inspector.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ante-mortem inspection

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 24 hours before the slaughter
  of a food animal other than a game animal and in accordance with the
  document entitled Ante-mortem Examination and Presentation Procedures
  for Food Animals, prepared by the Agency and published on its website,
  as amended from time to time, a licence holder must, for the purpose
  of an ante-mortem inspection, present the food animal or a sample from
  the shipment that the food animal is part of and, in the case of an
  equine or a bird other than an ostrich, rhea or emu, the documents
  referred to in subsection 165(1) to a veterinary inspector or
  inspector under the supervision of a veterinary inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deviations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an inspector who is not a veterinary inspector
  suspects that the food animal shows a deviation from normal behaviour,
  physiology or appearance, the licence holder must hold it for an
  inspection by a veterinary inspector.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-144, s. 10(E)](#1362156-1362749){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1362156-1362749.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20190115.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condemnation

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** If a veterinary
inspector, or an inspector under the supervision of a veterinary
inspector, determines after an inspection that any meat product that
would be derived from a food animal could not be identified as edible
under section 125 and condemns the food animal, a licence holder must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify the food animal as inedible;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} segregate the food animal with other condemned food
  animals or isolate the food animal if, because of its condition, it
  presents a risk to other food animals or it requires protection from
  other food animals;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} humanely kill the food animal; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} identify the carcass and any blood collected from the
  food animal as inedible.

#### [SUBDIVISION F]{.HLabel3}[Slaughtering and Dressing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844803 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement before bleeding

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** Before bleeding a food
animal, other than a game animal, a licence holder must render it
unconscious in a manner that prevents it from regaining consciousness
before death or slaughter it by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} delivering a blow to the head with a mechanical
  device in a manner that causes an immediate loss of consciousness;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} applying an electrical current in a manner that
  causes an immediate loss of consciousness; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} exposing it to a gas or a gas mixture in a manner
  that causes a rapid loss of consciousness.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement after bleeding starts

**[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
not cut the carcass of a food animal after bleeding has started if it
shows signs of sensibility that may indicate a potential return to
consciousness.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement before suspending

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must not suspend a food animal before it is rendered
  unconscious or slaughtered in accordance with section 141, before it
  is ritually slaughtered in accordance with section 144 or before it is
  humanely killed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- certain birds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a licence holder may suspend
  a bird, other than an ostrich, rhea or emu, by its legs immediately
  before it is rendered unconscious or slaughtered in accordance with
  section 141 or immediately before it is humanely killed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ritual slaughter

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 141, a
licence holder who ritually slaughters a food animal to comply with
Judaic or Islamic law must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} restrain the food animal;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} administer one continuous, fluid cut with a knife,
  without the knife being lifted off the food animal, resulting in the
  rapid, simultaneous and complete severance of the jugular veins and
  carotid arteries, in a manner that causes the animal to bleed
  immediately; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} rapidly and completely bleed it, to render it
  unconscious in a manner that prevents it from regaining consciousness
  before death.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dressing

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After a food animal is bled, a
  licence holder must dress the carcass by doing the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the carcass of a pig,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove the hair, scurf, toenails and developed
      mammary glands or remove the skin, head, developed mammary glands
      and feet at the carpal and tarsal joints, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} eviscerate and split it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the carcass of a bird,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove the feathers and hair or remove the skin,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} remove the head, uropygial gland and feet at the
      tarsal joints, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} eviscerate it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the carcass of a goat,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove the hair, head, toenails and developed
      mammary glands or remove the skin, head, developed mammary glands
      and feet at the carpal and tarsal joints, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} eviscerate it; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the carcass of any other food
    animal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove the skin, head, developed mammary glands
      and feet at the carpal and tarsal joints,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} eviscerate it, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} split it, except in the case of a sheep, calf
      or domesticated rabbit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Partial dressing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1) and at the request of the
  licence holder, the Minister must authorize the licence holder to
  partially dress a carcass if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder establishes that there is a
    market for partially dressed carcasses; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder's procedure for partial dressing
    is such that the carcass is sufficiently dressed to enable a
    post-mortem examination or inspection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Blood clots, bone splinters and
extraneous matter

**[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
remove any blood clot, bone splinter and extraneous matter from the
carcass of a food animal and the parts of the carcass, and must identify
what was removed as inedible.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processing of blood

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
process a food animal's blood in the inedible products area described in
subsection 58(2) unless the licence holder

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} collects it in a manner that prevents contamination;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} protects it against contamination after it is
  collected; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not defibrinate it by hand.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification and correlation

**[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
identify the blood of a food animal that is collected to be processed as
an edible meat product and the parts of the carcass of the food animal
in a manner that enables the correlation of the blood and the parts with
the carcass from which they were removed until the completion of the
post-mortem inspection or examination.

#### [SUBDIVISION G]{.HLabel3}[Post-mortem Inspection and Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844851 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Post-mortem inspection

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder must, during the course of dressing or partially
  dressing a carcass, present the carcass, its parts, and any blood of
  the food animal that is collected to be processed as an edible meat
  product to a veterinary inspector, or an inspector under the
  supervision of a veterinary inspector, for a post-mortem inspection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deviations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A licence holder, other than a licence holder who is
  authorized to conduct a post-mortem defect management program, must
  not, before the post-mortem inspection is completed, remove from the
  carcass any part that shows a deviation from normal appearance unless
  authorized to do so by a veterinary inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deviations --- post-mortem defect
  management program

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a licence holder who is authorized to
  conduct a post-mortem defect management program, the licence holder
  must not, before the post-mortem inspection begins, remove from the
  carcass any part that shows a deviation from normal appearance unless
  authorized to do so by a veterinary inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- post-mortem examination
  program

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to a licence holder who
  is authorized to conduct a post-mortem examination program under
  subsection 160(3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Post-mortem examination

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During the course of dressing or
  partially dressing a carcass, a licence holder who is authorized to
  conduct a post-mortem examination program under subsection 160(3)
  must, under the supervision of a veterinary inspector, conduct a
  post-mortem examination of the carcass, its parts and the blood of the
  food animal that has been collected to be processed as an edible meat
  product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Post-mortem defect management program

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} During the course of dressing or partially dressing a
  carcass, a licence holder who is authorized to conduct a post-mortem
  defect management program under subsection 160(3) must, under the
  supervision of a veterinary inspector, do the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the post-mortem inspection begins, conduct a
    post-mortem screening of the carcass, its parts and the blood of the
    food animal that has been collected to be processed as an edible
    meat product; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before the post-mortem inspection of the carcass is
    completed, implement the necessary measures with respect to any
    defects of the carcass or its parts.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection legend applied before
refrigeration

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** In the case of an
edible dressed or partially dressed whole carcass or an edible dressed
carcass side, other than a carcass or carcass side of a domesticated
rabbit or a bird that is not an ostrich, rhea or emu, the inspection
legend must be applied after the post-mortem inspection or examination
and before refrigeration

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by stamping it directly onto the carcass or carcass
  side; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} by applying a label to the carcass or carcass side
  that is shown prominently and that bears the inspection legend, the
  date of slaughter of the food animal from which the carcass or carcass
  side is derived and a code that enables the carcass or carcass side to
  be correlated with the slaughter of the food animal.

#### [SUBDIVISION H]{.HLabel3}[Inedible Meat Products]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844872 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condemnation

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** If a veterinary
inspector, or an inspector under the supervision of a veterinary
inspector, determines after a post-mortem inspection that any meat
product that would be derived from a carcass, any of its parts or the
blood of the food animal could not be identified as edible under section
125 and condemns the carcass, its parts or the blood of the food animal,
a licence holder must identify any meat product that is derived from the
condemned carcass, parts or blood as inedible.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
identify as inedible

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the carcass, any of its parts or the blood of a food
  animal that is rejected by the licence holder who is authorized to
  conduct a post-mortem examination program or a post-mortem defect
  management program under subsection 160(3); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the carcass of a food animal that dies other than by
  slaughter in accordance with these Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meat products treated as inedible

- **[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder may treat as inedible any meat product that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} does not have its movement restricted by an
    inspector; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has its movement restricted by an inspector if the
    licence holder obtains an inspector's authorization to move the meat
    product to the inedible products area described in subsection 58(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The licence holder must identify as inedible any meat
  product that the licence holder treats as inedible.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inedible products area

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a meat product is condemned or identified as inedible, a licence
  holder must move it directly to the inedible products area described
  in subsection 58(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Labelling and disposal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The licence holder must take one of the following
  measures in respect of a meat product that is moved to the inedible
  products area:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} apply or attach a label to it that indicates its
    intended use and bears the expression "Not for Use as Human Food" or
    "[ne peut servir à l'alimentation humaine]{.otherLang lang="fr"}";
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} dispose of it in accordance with section 66.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specified risk material

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), the licence holder must keep
  a meat product that is a specified risk material, contains a specified
  risk material or is derived from a specified risk material in a
  separate area of the inedible products area and must handle and
  destroy it in accordance with Part I.1 of the [Health of Animals
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._296).

#### [SUBDIVISION I]{.HLabel3}[Treatment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844895 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contaminated meat product

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder must
take one of the following measures in respect of a contaminated meat
product:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject it to a treatment or process that eliminates
  the contamination; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify it as inedible.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp.
--- pork

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder may
identify as edible a meat product that is derived from a pig and that
does not require further preparation before consumption, other than
washing or thawing or exposing it to sufficient heat to warm it without
cooking it, only if the conditions for identifying the meat product as
edible under section 125 are met and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the pork is subjected to a treatment or process that
  inactivates [Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp. viable larvae;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the pork is derived from a carcass that tests
  negative for the detection of [Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp.
  larvae using a method that is shown by evidence to be effective; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the pig originates from a farm that operates an
  on-farm food safety program under which the risk of
  [Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp. infection is negligible.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp.
--- equine

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder may
identify as edible a meat product that is derived from an equine only if
the conditions for identifying the meat product as edible under section
125 are met and the equine's carcass tests negative for the detection of
[Trichinella]{.otherLang lang="la"} spp. larvae using a method that is
shown by evidence to be effective.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bovine cysticercosis

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** A licence holder may
identify as edible a meat product that is derived from a bovine whose
carcass is affected by or shows evidence of bovine cysticercosis only if
the conditions for identifying the meat product as edible under section
125 are met and the licence holder has

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} removed the parts of the carcass that are affected
  and identified them as inedible; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subjected the remaining parts to a treatment or
  process that inactivates bovine cysticercosis viable larvae.

#### [SUBDIVISION J]{.HLabel3}[Post-mortem Programs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844911 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for an authorization

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a licence to slaughter may apply in writing to the Minister
  for an authorization to conduct

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a post-mortem examination program concerning a food
    animal referred to in the document entitled Fundamentals of the
    Post-mortem Examination Program, prepared by the Agency and
    published on its website, as amended from time to time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a post-mortem defect management program concerning
    a food animal referred to in the document entitled Fundamentals of
    the Post-mortem Defect Management Program prepared by the Agency and
    published on its website, as amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder's licence number and the address
    of the establishment where the program will be conducted; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the licence holder's preventive control
    plan that meets the requirements of section 89.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must authorize the licence holder to
  conduct a post-mortem examination program or a post-mortem defect
  management program if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the preventive control plan that is included in the
    licence holder's application meets the requirements of section 89;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion, based on the
    information that was made available to him or her, that the licence
    holder is able to conduct the program in compliance with these
    Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for suspension

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may
suspend a licence holder's authorization to conduct a post-mortem
examination program or a post-mortem defect management program if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder does not comply with their
  post-mortem examination program or post-mortem defect management
  program;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder does not comply with any provision
  of the Act or of these Regulations; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that a risk of injury
  to human health may result if the licence holder continues to conduct
  the program.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must not suspend a licence holder's authorization to conduct
  a post-mortem examination program or a post-mortem defect management
  program unless the licence holder

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was provided with an inspection report that sets
    out the grounds for the suspension and the period within which
    corrective action must be taken in order to avoid the suspension;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} failed to take corrective action within that
    period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must notify the licence holder in
  writing of the suspension and the date on which it takes effect.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk of injury to human health

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 162, if the Minister
  is of the opinion that a risk of injury to human health may result if
  the licence holder continues to conduct the post-mortem examination
  program or post-mortem defect management program, the Minister may
  suspend the authorization immediately after the licence holder is
  provided with an inspection report that sets out the grounds for the
  suspension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must notify the licence holder in
  writing that their authorization is suspended and that the suspension
  takes effect immediately.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension --- duration

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** The suspension of an
authorization to conduct a post-mortem examination program or
post-mortem defect management program must be lifted if the Minister
determines that corrective action has been taken.

#### [SUBDIVISION K]{.HLabel3}[Food Animal Information Documents and Document Keeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844944 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required documents

- **[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before slaughtering a food animal
  that is an equine or a bird, the holder of a licence to slaughter must
  obtain, from the person who owned or had the possession, care or
  control of the food animal before its arrival at the establishment
  where it is intended to be slaughtered, documents that include the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and contact information of the person who
    owned it and any person who had the possession, care or control of
    it immediately before its arrival at the establishment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the last location where it was raised or kept
    before its arrival at the establishment, including the address of
    the location, its code or the number that identifies it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the food animal's identification number or any
    other information that identifies it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of any on-farm food safety program under
    which the food animal was raised or kept;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a bird,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the time at which the first bird of the lot of
      birds was placed into a crate,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the time at which the bird was last provided
      with access to a source of hydration before being loaded, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the time at which the bird was last provided
      with access to feed before being loaded;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of any physical or chemical hazard to
    which the food animal may have been exposed that could cause any
    meat product that is derived from the food animal to be
    contaminated;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the last 120 days of the life of a
    bird that has been used for breeding or egg production or in respect
    of the entire life of any other bird,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the mortality rate for the flock from which the
      bird originates,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name of any disease or syndrome that was
      diagnosed in the flock from which the bird originates and the date
      on which the flock recovered from the disease or syndrome,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the names of any drugs administered to treat a
      disease or syndrome or administered as an extra-label use and of
      any vaccines that were administered to the bird, as well as

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} their route of administration,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the first and last date of their
        administration,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the dosage that was administered, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} the withdrawal period or, in the case of an
        extra-label drug administration, a copy of the prescription that
        was issued by a veterinarian and an attestation by a competent
        person or body with respect to the withdrawal period for that
        administration, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the name of any drug that has been administered
      in the last 14 days that requires a withdrawal period; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the last 180 days of the life of an
    equine,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name of any disease or syndrome that was
      diagnosed or a description of any deviation from normal behaviour,
      physiology or appearance,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the names of any drugs and vaccines that were
      administered to the equine, as well as

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} their Drug Identification Numbers, if any,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} their route of administration,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the last date of their administration,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} the dosage that was administered, and

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} the withdrawal period or, in the case of an
        extra-label drug administration, a copy of the prescription that
        was issued by a veterinarian and an attestation by a competent
        person or body with respect to the withdrawal period for that
        administration, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the use of the equine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- equines and birds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1) and subsections 138(1) and
  139(1), the licence holder may slaughter the equine or bird without
  having first obtained the documents referred to in subsection (1) if
  they notify an inspector before slaughtering the equine or bird and
  if, subsequent to the slaughter, the meat product that is derived from
  the equine or bird

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is held by the licence holder until they have
    obtained those documents, examined them and presented them to a
    veterinary inspector or to an inspector under the supervision of a
    veterinary inspector; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is identified as inedible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- game animal, ostrich,
  rhea and emu

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a game
  animal or an ostrich, rhea or emu.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention period of documents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The documents referred to in subsection (1) must be
  kept for one year after the day on which the food animal arrives at
  the establishment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document keeping

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a licence to slaughter must prepare and keep documents that
  set out the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a food animal that is slaughtered,
    the date and time that it was slaughtered and the findings of the
    ante-mortem examination and, if the licence holder is authorized to
    conduct a post-mortem examination program or a post-mortem defect
    management program, the findings of the post-mortem examination or
    the post-mortem screening and the reason for any condemnation or
    rejection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a food animal that is found dead at
    the time of its arrival at the establishment or that dies in the
    establishment other than by slaughter in accordance with these
    Regulations,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and time that the food animal was found
      dead or was humanely killed,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} its identification number or any other
      information that identifies it, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the details of its disposal; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to the food animals referred to in
    paragraph (b), the total number per day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention period of documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The documents referred to in subsection (1) must be
  kept for one year after the day on which the food animal arrives at
  the establishment.

#### [SUBDIVISION L]{.HLabel3}[Import and Export]{.HTitleText3} {#h-844994 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** The holder of a licence
to import may import an edible meat product only if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign state in which the meat product is
  manufactured, prepared, stored, packaged or labelled, as the case may
  be, has, at the time the activity is conducted, an inspection system
  for meat products that is recognized under Part 7;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign state from which the meat product is
  imported has, at the time of the import, an inspection system for meat
  products that is recognized under Part 7;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the establishment where the food animal from which
  the meat product is derived was slaughtered, and any establishment
  where the meat product was manufactured, processed, treated,
  preserved, handled, tested, graded, coded, stored, packaged or
  labelled, have, at the time that the activity is conducted and at the
  time of the import, a system for manufacturing, processing, treating,
  preserving, handling, testing, grading, coding, slaughtering, storing,
  packaging or labelling, as the case may be, that is recognized under
  Part 7; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder provides an inspector with an
  official document issued by the foreign state, in a form approved by
  the President, that states that the meat product meets the
  requirements that are set out in the Act and these Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence holder may export an edible meat product only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the licence holder provides an inspector with a
    document that substantiates that the requirements of the foreign
    state to which it is intended to be exported are met in respect of
    that meat product; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate or other document is issued under
    section 48 of the Act in respect of that meat product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- removal notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a meat
  product that is subject to a notice ordering its removal from Canada
  under subsection 32(1) of the Act.

## [PART 7]{.HLabel1}[Recognition of Foreign Systems]{.HTitleText1} {#h-845008 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception ---
[[shellfish]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of the adductor muscles of scallops and the meat of
geoducks.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for recognition of inspection
system

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign state may make an application to the Minister in writing for
  the recognition of its inspection system for meat products or live or
  raw shellfish.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inspection system for meat
    products,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the species of birds or mammals
      and a description of the meat products to which the system
      applies, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the approximate number of establishments where
      the manufacturing, preparing, storing, packaging or labelling of
      meat products that are intended to be exported to Canada would be
      conducted and an indication of the activities that would be
      conducted in those establishments;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inspection system for live or raw
    shellfish, an indication of the species and the growing and
    harvesting areas to which the system applies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the volume of meat products or live or raw
    shellfish to which the system applies that is anticipated to be
    exported to Canada per year;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the items set out in paragraph (3)(a) or (b), as
    the case may be; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name, title and signature of the authorized
    representative of the foreign state who makes the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recognition by Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must recognize the inspection system in
  respect of which the application is made if the system provides at
  least the same level of protection as that provided by the provisions
  of the Act and these Regulations, taking into account the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inspection system for meat
    products,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any applicable legislative framework, controls
      and procedures,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the organizational structure of the authority
      that is responsible for the system,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of the system,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the resources that support the objectives of the
      system,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the humane treatment of the food animals that are
      intended to be slaughtered,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the chemical residue monitoring and
      microbiological monitoring of the meat products,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the certification process for the export of the
      meat products, and

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inspection system for live or raw
    shellfish,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any applicable legislative framework, controls
      and procedures,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the organizational structure of the authority
      that is responsible for the system,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of the system,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the resources that support the objectives of the
      system,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the chemical and microbiological monitoring of
      the shellfish, including monitoring for biotoxins,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the monitoring of the waters in the growing and
      harvesting areas to assess their suitability for their intended
      purpose, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1362158-1362752.html"
  role="button"}
:::
